People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Well, hi, Doctor. It's really nice to meet you. Um... I guess I'm just here because, well, my husband and the folks down at the warehouse where I work, they've been... well, they've been acting a little concerned about me. They keep acting like I'm, you know, not making sense or something, which I hope isn't true. I always try to do a good job. 

But, um... it's just that I've noticed some things at work. I don't want to sound ungrateful for my job, but I know they've been watching my moves pretty closely. And... well, they've been changing the pallet counts on me. I really think it's just to keep me from getting that supervisor spot I've been working so hard for. Does that make sense? I mean, I'm not trying to cause trouble or anything, I just... I just want to do my job right.

Um... yes, I think that kind of goes with what I was saying before about being fired up. I hope I'm not over-explaining things, I just want to make sure you have the whole picture.

It makes me a really good worker and a good mom, keeping the house so clean, right? I mean, I know it's a lot of activity, but it's not a bad thing to be energetic, is it? I hope that's the kind of example you were looking for.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... yeah, I guess so. I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I'm a mean person or anything, because I'm really not. I try so hard to be a good wife and mom. 

But... yeah, there are days, or even a whole week, where I just feel... fired up. Like nobody can slow me down, and I can just take on the whole world. It feels really good, honestly. But... well, the only bad part is that sometimes, if my husband just asks a tiny little question—like about the kids or what time I'm working—I just... I snap at him. I don't mean to! It just feels like he's trying to control me or slow me down when I'm on a roll. 

I always feel bad about it later. I know it's not fair to him. Is that... is that normal to feel that way sometimes? I hope I'm not making myself sound awful. I'm just trying to be honest with you.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no, no. I've never had to go to the hospital for anything like that. I mean, I get a little keyed up sometimes, but it's never been so bad that I couldn't handle it at home, you know? I always manage to keep things under control before it gets to that point.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, hi, Doctor. It's really nice to meet you. Um... I guess I'm just here because, well, my husband and the folks down at the warehouse where I work, they've been... well, they've been acting a little concerned about me. They keep acting like I'm, you know, not making sense or something, which I hope isn't true. I always try to do a good job. \n\nBut, um... it's just that I've noticed some things at work. I don't want to sound ungrateful for my job, but I know they've been watching my moves pretty closely. And... well, they've been changing the pallet counts on me. I really think it's just to keep me from getting that supervisor spot I've been working so hard for. Does that make sense? I mean, I'm not trying to cause trouble or anything, I just... I just want to do my job right.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these concerns about people at work watching you and changing the pallet counts?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's been going on for a little while now. Maybe... about eight months? Give or take. That's when I first started noticing the pallet counts being messed with, and feeling like everyone was watching me so closely. \n\nAnd... well, I know you didn't ask this, and I really hope it doesn't make me sound bad, but... that's also around the time I started hearing the voices, too. Just a little bit, though! I try really, really hard to just ignore them and keep my head down so I can do a good job on the floor. I hope it's okay that I waited this long to say something. Eight months isn't too bad to wait, right? I just didn't want anyone to think I couldn't handle my shifts.\n\nDoctor: During the past eight months, have you had a time when you felt unusually depressed or unusually high/energized for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess there have been some days like that. I really don't want you to think I'm hard to be around or anything, but... sometimes I do get this feeling where I'm just so fired up, you know? Like I have all this energy and absolutely nobody can slow me down. I get so much done at work those days. \n\nBut, um... the bad part, and I feel really awful admitting this... is that sometimes when I feel like that, if my husband just asks me one tiny little question about the kids or, you know, my work schedule... I just snap at him. It feels like he's trying to control me or something, even though I know he's just asking. I always apologize after, I really do. I try to be a good wife. Is it normal to get a little snappy sometimes when you have a lot of energy? I hope that doesn't make me sound like a terrible person.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices or the belief that people at work are watching you and changing things ever happened when your mood felt completely normal?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yeah, I guess they have. I really hope that doesn't mean I'm getting worse or anything. But... there was a time, maybe a few weeks, where things were a lot quieter for me. I was sleeping a lot closer to normal, you know, and I wasn't racing around with all that energy or snapping at my husband. I felt... pretty okay, really. \n\nBut, um... even then, I still heard the voice. Just... just telling me to check things, you know? And I still knew, deep down, that the pallet counts were being changed on me at the warehouse. I tried not to let it bother me too much, since I was feeling better otherwise. That's okay, right? That I still noticed those things even when my mood was fine? I just... I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one week when you felt so unusually high, energized, or irritable that you needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I suppose there have been times like that. I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm neglecting my health or anything, because I really do try to take care of myself. But... yeah, there have been weeks where I sleep maybe... just three hours a night? \n\nI'd wake up way before my alarm even went off, and the funny thing is, I wouldn't even feel tired! I'd just feel like I didn't need any more rest at all to get moving and start my day. I'd just get right up and go. I mean, getting by on a little less sleep sometimes just means you're a hard worker, right? A lot of people do that. I just want to make sure you don't think I'm doing something wrong by not sleeping as much on those days.\n\nDoctor: Have there ever been times when you were hearing voices or feeling watched even though you were not having any manic or depressed symptoms at all?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes. I think... well, like I was just saying a little bit ago, I hope I explained it okay the first time. I really want to make sure I'm giving you what you need. But yes, there was that time... those quieter few weeks I mentioned? \n\nWhen I wasn't having any of those big energy spikes or feeling down at all, I was sleeping so much closer to a normal schedule. I wasn't racing around the warehouse or getting snappy with anyone. I felt really steady, which is a good thing, right? I was really proud of how I was doing.\n\nBut, um... yeah, even during that really good, normal time, I still heard the voice telling me to double-check things. And I still knew they were messing with my pallet counts at work. It didn't go away just because my mood was better. I hope you don't mind me repeating myself, since I know I kind of just brought that up. Am I answering your questions right? I just really want to make sure I'm being a good patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one week when you felt so unusually high or irritable that you were much more active or talked much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yeah, I guess there are times like that. I hope it doesn't make me sound like a bad person or a bad mom or anything. But sometimes... I just feel really fired up, you know? Like nobody can slow me down and I can just get so much done. It's actually kind of nice to feel that productive, right? \n\nBut, um... the talking part, and the irritable part... yeah. If I'm feeling like that, and my husband just asks one small question—like about the kids, or what my schedule is for the week—I just... I snap at him. It feels like he's trying to control me or slow me down when I'm just trying to get things done. I always feel awful about it later, because I know he's just trying to help. I really do try to be a good wife. I hope you don't think I'm just a mean person for getting snappy like that. Is it normal to feel that way sometimes when you have a lot of energy?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one week when your mood was so elevated, expansive, or irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I think that's kind of what I was just trying to explain. I hope it's okay that I already brought it up... I'm just trying to give you the right answers. \n\nBut yes, there are those weeks where I just feel so... elevated, I guess? Like I'm just so fired up and absolutely nobody can slow me down. I feel so capable, which is a good thing, right? It's nice to feel that way instead of being down. \n\nBut then... well, like I mentioned, my mood can get really irritable during those same weeks. If my husband just asks me one tiny little question about the kids, or what my schedule is at work, I just... I snap at him. It feels like he's trying to control me or get in my way when I'm just trying to be productive. It's definitely different from how I usually am, because normally I'm really patient with him and the kids. I really am a good mom, I promise. I just... I get so easily frustrated when I'm feeling that high. Does that make sense? Am I explaining it the way you need me to?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever gone at least two weeks in a row with voices or paranoid beliefs continuing even while your mood was completely normal?\n\nPatient: Um... yes. I think... well, I know we just talked about this a little bit, and I really hope I'm not being annoying by repeating myself. I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need for your notes. \n\nBut yes, there was that time... um, those quieter few weeks I mentioned earlier. My mood was completely fine. I wasn't having those big energy spikes, and I wasn't feeling down at all. I was sleeping so much closer to a normal schedule, which was really nice, you know? I wasn't racing around the warehouse or getting snappy with my husband. I felt really steady and good. \n\nBut even then, during that really normal time... I still heard the voice. The one telling me to double-check things. And I still knew, without a doubt, that they were messing with my pallet counts at work. It didn't stop just because my mood was better. I hope it's okay that I brought that up again... I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right. Am I doing okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one week when you felt unusually high, energized, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I think that kind of goes with what I was saying before about being fired up. I hope I'm not over-explaining things, I just want to make sure you have the whole picture. \n\nBut yeah, during those weeks, I just get so much energy in my body. It's actually a really good thing for my job, to be honest! Like, I'll finish a full shift at the warehouse—moving freight and everything—and instead of being tired like a normal person, I'll go to my boxing class, come home, scrub the entire kitchen, and then start making all these lists for the next day. I just... I don't even feel ready to sit down or rest. \n\nIt makes me a really good worker and a good mom, keeping the house so clean, right? I mean, I know it's a lot of activity, but it's not a bad thing to be energetic, is it? I hope that's the kind of example you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Have there ever been at least two weeks when the voices or the belief that people were watching or changing things continued even though your mood was completely normal?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I know we just touched on that, so I'm sorry if I'm sounding like a broken record. I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need for your file. \n\nBut yes, there was that time... um, a few weeks where things were a lot quieter. My mood was really good, actually. Completely normal. I wasn't having those huge bursts of energy, and I wasn't feeling sad at all. I was sleeping so much better, closer to a normal schedule, which is a good thing, right? I wasn't racing around the warehouse or getting snappy with anyone. \n\nBut... even then, when I was feeling so steady and normal... I still heard the voice. The one telling me to double-check my work. And I still knew, deep down, that they were messing with my pallet counts. It didn't go away just because my mood was better. I hope it's okay that I brought that up again... I just want to be helpful. Am I giving you the right kind of answers?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a full week where your mood was so elevated or irritable that you felt unusually grand or more confident than usual?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess so. I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I'm a mean person or anything, because I'm really not. I try so hard to be a good wife and mom. \n\nBut... yeah, there are days, or even a whole week, where I just feel... fired up. Like nobody can slow me down, and I can just take on the whole world. It feels really good, honestly. But... well, the only bad part is that sometimes, if my husband just asks a tiny little question—like about the kids or what time I'm working—I just... I snap at him. I don't mean to! It just feels like he's trying to control me or slow me down when I'm on a roll. \n\nI always feel bad about it later. I know it's not fair to him. Is that... is that normal to feel that way sometimes? I hope I'm not making myself sound awful. I'm just trying to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Are you having one of those high-energy, irritable periods right now?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, definitely not right now. I feel pretty steady today. I'm just sitting here, trying my best to answer your questions right. I don't feel that huge rush of energy at all right now, and I haven't been snapping at my husband or anyone else lately, I promise. \n\nI try really hard to just be calm and polite, especially when I'm talking to doctors like you. I hope I'm coming across okay? Like I said before, I really don't want you to think I'm just this angry or out-of-control person all the time. I'm usually very level-headed. Am I doing okay with these questions? I just want to make sure I'm being a good patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Have any of your high-energy periods ever caused you to be hospitalized?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. I've never had to go to the hospital for anything like that. I mean, I get a little keyed up sometimes, but it's never been so bad that I couldn't handle it at home, you know? I always manage to keep things under control before it gets to that point. \n\nI really try to stay on top of things so I don't become a burden to my family or, um, take up a hospital bed when someone else might need it more. So, no hospital stays for being too energetic or anything. That's a good thing, right? I hope that means I'm doing a decent job managing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one week when your symptoms were so severe that they caused major problems with work, relationships, or taking care of yourself?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart or anything, because I really do try my best. But, um... yeah, I guess there have been times where things got a little messy. \n\nLike at work... I work in a warehouse, moving materials around. And, um, my supervisors actually had to take me off the forklift and some of the other safety-sensitive stuff. I guess my counts were getting a little mixed up, and my routines were... well, they said I was slowing the line down. I felt so bad about it. I really didn't mean to cause any trouble for the team, you know? I always want to be a good worker. \n\nAnd then at home... my husband, he's been so patient with me, but he did mention that he feels like he can't rely on me the way he used to. That really hurts to hear, because I want to be a good wife and mom more than anything. I just... I get so overwhelmed sometimes, and things slip through the cracks. \n\nIs it okay that I'm telling you this? I know it doesn't make me look very good, but I'm just trying to be honest so you can help me. I really am trying my hardest, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when the voices or paranoia were present for at least two weeks while you were not having any manic or depressive symptoms at all?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I want to make sure I'm answering this right for you. I guess... yes? There was a time, maybe a few weeks, where I actually felt pretty okay. Like, I was sleeping a lot closer to normal, and I wasn't rushing around or feeling all that extra energy. I thought I was doing really good, honestly. \n\nBut, um... even during that quiet time, I still... I still heard that voice. The one telling me I needed to keep checking things. And I still really felt like someone was messing with my pallet counts at the warehouse on purpose, just to make me look bad. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds strange. I really hope you don't think I'm crazy for saying that. I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information. Does that make sense? Did I answer that the way you needed me to?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing voices or feeling paranoid, while you were not having any manic or depressed symptoms at all?\n\nPatient: I know we just talked about this, and I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much... I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need. But, um, yes. Like I was saying, there was that quiet time—maybe a couple of weeks or so? I was actually doing pretty well with my sleep, and I wasn't all wound up or racing around. I felt almost... normal, I guess? \n\nBut even then, when I was feeling so much better, I still heard that voice telling me I had to keep checking things. And I still really felt like someone was messing with my pallet counts at work just to get me in trouble. I'm sorry if that sounds silly or if I'm not explaining it right. I just... I want to make sure I'm answering your question properly. Is that what you were asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Like I'm just so fired up and absolutely nobody can slow me down. I feel so capable, which is a good thing, right?
